========== FILE: ./docs/notation.md ==========
# Pipeline Notation

[docs/pipeline.md](pipeline.md) describes this repo's own pipeline as a
sequence of resource equations. This document is the language that
description is written in — read it before or alongside the pipeline
page if the equation syntax there is unfamiliar.

The notation exists in this repo for a specific reason: sessions
working on this codebase can't see each other, so a pipeline stage
authored by one session has to be checkable and alignable by the next
without either session holding the other's context. Writing a stage as
an equation — fixed inputs, fixed outputs, a named law — turns "does
this stage still do what it's supposed to" into something a later
session (or a reader) can verify by inspection, not something that has
to be remembered or re-explained. General notation — equation syntax,
diagram conventions, the monoidal-category reading — is the
`.agents/skills/string-diagram` skill's job; this document defines
only what's specific to *this* repo's pipeline, not the notation
itself.

## Equation form

An equation names one pipeline stage:

```
inputs → outputs  [Stage]  {catalytic: …}
```

Read left to right: `inputs` are consumed, `outputs` are produced,
`[Stage]` names the operation, and `{catalytic: …}` (when present)
names resources that participate without being consumed. `×` (or the
ASCII fallback `*`) joins multiple inputs; `+` joins multiple outputs.

## Resource names bind to types

Every resource name in an equation binds to a concrete type:

- **Data resources** bind to a Malli schema — a resource named
  `fhir-datum` means "a value that validates against the schema
  documented at its point of use," not a free-floating name.
- **Prose resources** (protocols, results files, capability doc pages)
  bind to a template/rubric pair (pattern nursery
  [#12](../.agents/memory/patterns.md)) — the prose complement of
  Malli, since a protocol document can't be hard-schema'd but can
  still be scored against a fixed rubric.

An equation whose resource name binds to neither is malformed —
authoring the equation is what forces this binding to be made
explicit, before implementation.

## Catalytic resources resolve to one of four targets

A resource marked `{catalytic: …}` participates in a stage without
being consumed (pattern nursery [#13](../.agents/memory/patterns.md)).
Every catalytic resource must resolve to exactly one of four targets:

1. `artifacts.lock.edn` — an acquired, external, or binary input
   (ADR-0005): an engine distribution, a runtime, a profile package.
2. `deps.edn` — a JVM/Clojure library dependency.
3. Hashed repo-authored config — text this repo wrote and versions
   (a properties file, a module set), referenced by path plus
   content-hash at run time.
4. **In-repo code registries**, referenced by `{id, version}` — a
   versioned catalog of code this repo authors and ships as part of
   itself (the operator catalog, `corpus.operators`; the canonicalizer
   registry, `corpus.canonicalizers`). Distinguished from target 3 by
   kind, not degree: target 3 is data this repo wrote; target 4 is code
   this repo wrote, versioned and referenced the same way data would
   be. (This target was added to close a gap in the original three —
   see pattern nursery [#13](../.agents/memory/patterns.md) for the
   trial history that surfaced it.)

A catalytic resource that resolves to none of the four is a gap in
the equation, not an oversight to paper over silently.

## The five stage kinds

Every stage in this repo's pipeline is one of five kinds, each with a
one-line law:

| Kind | Law |
|---|---|
| **transform** | Appends provenance — never drops what came before. |
| **normalize** | Is an idempotent endomorphism — precisely a registered canonicalizer (pattern [#3](../.agents/memory/patterns.md)), not a separate concept. |
| **enrich** | Adds fields without altering existing ones. |
| **gate** | Splits its output into pass / rejected / indeterminate and never modifies the datum it judges. |
| **feedback** | Carries a round bound — no unbounded loops. |

A stage may declare laws beyond its kind's own — kind laws are a
floor, not a ceiling. A stage-specific law is anything the stage's own
equation or accompanying prose asserts that a generic stage of that
kind wouldn't otherwise guarantee (e.g. Mutate's intended-diff-only
invariant: the canonicalized diff between base and mutant touches
exactly the declared locator/contract target and nothing else — true
of this particular `transform`, not of `transform` stages in general).

## Resource contracts

A resource may carry a precondition beyond its type — e.g. a parser's
round-trip fidelity (EXP-B2). Such a contract is verified by
experiment, not asserted by fiat, and an equation that depends on it
cites the experiment that verified it (e.g. "Mutate operates on
plain-data JSON because EXP-B2 verified its round-trip is faithful,"
not merely "plain-data JSON is what we use").

## Status

This formalization is exploratory, not settled: it is on trial against
the promotion criteria recorded in pattern nursery
[#13](../.agents/memory/patterns.md), and its shape may still change as
more stages move from planned to built. The equation syntax and
diagram-rendering machinery belong to the shared
`.agents/skills/string-diagram` skill (a general monoidal-category
notation, not authored for this repo specifically); what's specific
here is only the pipeline vocabulary this document defines.

Both this notation and the `string-diagram` rendering skill originate in
the author's [cyberneutics](https://github.com/pragsmike/cyberneutics)
methodology — the upstream skill (name and description match the copy
here) lives at
[`.claude/skills/string-diagram/SKILL.md`](https://github.com/pragsmike/cyberneutics/blob/main/.claude/skills/string-diagram/SKILL.md)
in that repo (retrieved HTTP 200, 2026-07-24). Formalization here remains
exploratory, as above.

========== END FILE ==========

{:schema-version 1
 :stages
 [{:id :generate
   :label "Generate"
   :kind :transform
   :status :built
   :inputs ["synthea-config"]
   :outputs ["raw-corpus"]
   :catalytic ["synthea-artifact" "jdk-runtime" "config-hash"]
   :laws ["appends a manifest (v1.1) recording every pinned input: generator artifact, JVM runtime artifact, seeds, engine params, config hash, forced environment"]
   :contract "byte-identical output modulo two registered canonicalizers, verified by EXP-A4"}

  {:id :normalize
   :label "Normalize"
   :kind :normalize
   :status :built
   :inputs ["raw-corpus"]
   :outputs ["canonical-fhir-datum"]
   :laws ["is the application of a registered canonicalizer (ehr-testing-tools.canonical) -- idempotence is the canonicalizer's own property-tested law, not a separate assertion here"]}

  {:id :mutate
   :label "Mutate"
   :kind :transform
   :status :built
   :inputs ["canonical-fhir-datum"]
   :outputs ["mutant-fhir-datum" "lineage-record"]
   :catalytic ["operator-catalog"]
   :laws ["appends provenance: every mutant carries a lineage record, never produced without one"
          "intended-diff-only: diff(canon(base), canon(mutant)) touches exactly the declared locator/contract target and nothing else"]
   :contract "operates on plain-data JSON (data.json), not a HAPI-parsed tree, because EXP-B2 verified plain-data JSON's parse-serialize round-trip is faithful"}

  {:id :gate
   :label "Gate"
   :kind :gate
   :status :planned
   :inputs ["fhir-datum"]
   :outputs ["pass" "rejected" "indeterminate"]
   :catalytic ["profile-artifact"]
   :laws ["never modifies the datum it judges"]}

  {:id :report
   :label "Report"
   :kind :transform
   :status :planned
   :inputs ["pass" "rejected" "indeterminate"]
   :outputs ["report"]
   :laws ["appends provenance: which engine, which profile, which verdicts, normalized to the finding envelope (pattern nursery #6)"]}]}

========== FILE: ./docs/pipeline.md ==========
<!-- GENERATED by `make pipeline` from docs/pipeline.edn -- do not hand-edit.
     Edit docs/pipeline.edn and regenerate instead. -->

# Pipeline

This is the pipeline this repo builds: a fixed sequence of stages that turns a Synthea configuration into a mutated, gate-ready corpus. Each stage below is a resource equation -- inputs consumed, outputs produced, and a `{catalytic: ...}` set naming resources the stage uses without consuming (an engine artifact, a runtime, a versioned operator catalog); see [docs/notation.md](notation.md) for what that notation means before reading the equations cold.

The equations are the source of truth: [docs/pipeline.edn](pipeline.edn). A stage marked `# planned` below is designed but not yet built -- its equation and law are fixed, its implementation isn't.

## Equations

```
synthea-config × synthea-artifact × jdk-runtime × config-hash → raw-corpus  [Generate]  {catalytic: synthea-artifact, jdk-runtime, config-hash}
raw-corpus → canonical-fhir-datum  [Normalize]
canonical-fhir-datum × operator-catalog → mutant-fhir-datum + lineage-record  [Mutate]  {catalytic: operator-catalog}
# planned: Gate
fhir-datum × profile-artifact → pass + rejected + indeterminate  [Gate]  {catalytic: profile-artifact}
# planned: Report
pass × rejected × indeterminate → report  [Report]
```

## Diagram

```mermaid
flowchart LR

    %% --- Source types (raw inputs, not produced by any operation) ---
    config_hash(["config-hash"])
    fhir_datum(["fhir-datum"])
    jdk_runtime(["jdk-runtime"])
    operator_catalog(["operator-catalog"])
    profile_artifact(["profile-artifact"])
    synthea_artifact(["synthea-artifact"])
    synthea_config(["synthea-config"])

    %% --- Operations (boxes; spiders use distinct shapes) ---
    Generate["Generate"]
    Normalize["Normalize"]
    Mutate["Mutate"]
    Gate["Gate"]
    Report["Report"]

    %% --- Wires (typed connections) ---
    %% Arrow 1: Generate
    synthea_config -- synthea-config --> Generate
    synthea_artifact -. synthea-artifact .-> Generate
    jdk_runtime -. jdk-runtime .-> Generate
    config_hash -. config-hash .-> Generate

    %% Arrow 2: Normalize
    Generate -- raw-corpus --> Normalize

    %% Arrow 3: Mutate
    Normalize -- canonical-fhir-datum --> Mutate
    operator_catalog -. operator-catalog .-> Mutate

    %% Arrow 5: Gate
    fhir_datum -- fhir-datum --> Gate
    profile_artifact -. profile-artifact .-> Gate

    %% Arrow 7: Report
    Gate -- pass --> Report
    Gate -- rejected --> Report
    Gate -- indeterminate --> Report

    %% --- Styling ---

    %% Operations: dark boxes (fan=blue, funnel=green, enrichment=outlined, gate=purple)
    style Generate fill:#2d2d2d,stroke:#000,color:#fff,stroke-width:2px
    style Normalize fill:#2d2d2d,stroke:#000,color:#fff,stroke-width:2px
    style Mutate fill:#2d2d2d,stroke:#000,color:#fff,stroke-width:2px
    style Gate fill:#2d2d2d,stroke:#000,color:#fff,stroke-width:2px
    style Report fill:#2d2d2d,stroke:#000,color:#fff,stroke-width:2px

    %% Source types: light rounded
    style config_hash fill:#f5f5f5,stroke:#999,color:#333
    style fhir_datum fill:#f5f5f5,stroke:#999,color:#333
    style jdk_runtime fill:#f5f5f5,stroke:#999,color:#333
    style operator_catalog fill:#f5f5f5,stroke:#999,color:#333
    style profile_artifact fill:#f5f5f5,stroke:#999,color:#333
    style synthea_artifact fill:#f5f5f5,stroke:#999,color:#333
    style synthea_config fill:#f5f5f5,stroke:#999,color:#333
```

========== FILE: ./docs/positioning.md ==========
# Positioning

This document maps how `ehr-testing-tools` relates to
[`ehr-testing-guide`](https://github.com/pragsmike/ehr-testing-guide) and
to the people who will encounter one or the other first. It is a working
doc, revisited as the repo's shape firms up — not a manifesto. It also
holds the go-public gate this repo set for itself and the record of
walking it: this repo has been public since [ADR-0008](../notes/ADRs.md)
(publication is a visibility change, not a release — see "Go-public gate
vs. first release" below).

## Audience

Three segments arrive here with different on-ramps:

1. **Guide readers, arriving method-first.** They've read (or are reading)
   the guide's account of corpus construction and conformance gating and
   want to know which tool serves which chapter. They need a map from
   method to capability, not a re-explanation of the method.
2. **Practitioners, arriving task-first.** They need test ADT messages, or
   they need to validate a bundle against a profile, and found this repo
   before the guide. They need task-oriented usage docs first, and a
   pointer to the guide second — once the task is done, the "why" behind
   it is worth reading. The first trial cohort (2026-07-24) sharpens this
   segment: EHR domain experts comfortable with Python but not Clojure,
   working agent-assisted (ChatGPT, Codex), largely on Windows 11/WSL2 —
   `SETUP.md` exists for exactly this cohort.
3. **Contributors.** They need the scope fence up front: PRs that add
   method content (new properties, new correctness arguments, new test-plan
   guidance) belong in the guide, not here. This repo's contribution
   surface is tool code, not method.

## The constellation

Three objects, three jobs:

- **The guide** teaches the method — what correctness means for a lossy
  transform, how a corpus should be layered, where validation sits
  relative to semantic checks.
- **The guide's `companion/`** is illustrative code, pinned to the book's
  prose, that exists to be read alongside a specific chapter. It is not
  meant to be depended on as a library.
- **This repo** is operational tooling with its own release clock. It
  exists to be run — as a CLI, as a library dependency, in CI.

Placement rule for any new artifact, in either project: **if its value is
explanatory, it belongs in the guide or its companion; if its value is
execution, it belongs here.**

## Referral triggers: guide → tools

The method demands a runnable capability at specific points. These are the
moments the guide will eventually point here:

- **Corpus construction** (guide ch. 23: generation, mutation, provenance
  manifests) — the generated and mutation layers of the corpus need a
  generator and a mutator; this repo supplies them.
- **Conformance gates** (guide ch. 25: v2 and FHIR validation as structural
  gates upstream of semantic checks) — this repo supplies the gate
  wrappers.
- **Data sources for the guide's own experiments** — notably, the guide's
  Experiment 3 will eventually consume this repo's v2 projector output.

These become register entries and cross-references in the guide **only
after this repo's first release** (a version tag and published
coordinates — see "Go-public gate vs. first release" below; this repo
being publicly visible is not the trigger). Nothing in the guide points
here yet, and that's deliberate — the guide should never cite tooling
that doesn't exist yet as though it does.

## Referral triggers: tools → guide

- This README's relationship section.
- Each tool's own docs, once written, linking the chapter that motivates
  its existence.
- A standing norm for issues filed against this repo: a question about
  method — "what properties should I write?", "what should my corpus
  contain?" — gets answered with a chapter link, not a docs expansion.
  That boundary is what keeps this repo from slowly reabsorbing the
  guide's scope one helpful paragraph at a time.

## Versioning and citation contract

This repo cites guide chapters by stable anchors. The guide, once it
starts citing this repo, will do so by version, with the same
"entry-last-verified" discipline it already applies to its own reference
part. Releases here declare which guide edition's method they implement
("implements the method as of guide vN").

Each tool carries a maturity label in its own docs — **experimental →
usable → stable** — once tool docs exist. The guide registers only
released, non-experimental tools; "experimental" is not citable.

## Dogfooding commitments

A reproducibility toolkit that doesn't practice reproducibility on itself
is self-refuting. This repo commits to:

- A facts register from day one (`notes/facts-register.md`, per
  [ADR-0002](../notes/ADRs.md)).
- Reproducibility manifests for everything generated (`corpus.manifest`
  schema v1, EXP-A4).
- Pinned dependencies throughout.
- CI that runs offline (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`, verified hermetic —
  see [ADR-0008](../notes/ADRs.md)).

## Go-public gate

The repo flipped from private to public when all four of the following
held. [ADR-0008](../notes/ADRs.md) is the record of that decision and
walks each condition against its evidence; this section is now a record
of the gate as designed, not a future condition — read ADR-0008 for how
each one was actually met.

1. **Licensing is clean** — the [ADR-0007](../notes/ADRs.md) MIT target
   is met (ADR-0007 supersedes ADR-0001's Apache-2.0 target; EXP-SBOM's
   compatibility findings were evaluated against "permissive
   open-source distribution" and transfer unchanged), every dependency
   is verified compatible, and EXP-SBOM (see `docs/experiments.md`) has
   resolved, including [F1](../notes/facts-register.md) (NIST
   v2-validation licensing). Artifacts this repo redistributes must be
   fully license-verified; artifacts users fetch at their own initiative
   from an official source ([ADR-0005](../notes/ADRs.md)'s 2026-07-24
   amendment) clear this gate on the weaker
   `:use-permitted--unstated--confirmation-pending` status instead.
2. At least one tool has reached "usable" maturity with honest docs (no
   overclaiming what it does).
3. CI is green and runs offline.
4. The referral README (the relationship section above, in shipped form)
   is in place.

## Go-public gate vs. first release

Publication and release are different events, and this repo is
deliberately public before it has released anything. Publication
(ADR-0008) is a visibility change: the four conditions above are about
whether the repo is honest and safe to look at, not about whether any
capability is finished. First release is a separate, later milestone —
a version tag, Clojars/Maven Central coordinates, and the point at which
the guide's own register may cite this repo (see "Referral triggers:
guide → tools" above, which still applies unchanged: nothing in the
guide points here until *release*, not merely publication). Until first
release: no version tag, no published artifacts, and every maturity
label in `README.md` still means what it says — "usable" is not
"stable," and pre-release expectations govern.

## Open decisions

Dated so staleness is visible; both resolved no later than generator
kickoff.

- **Internal repo organization** — resolved by
  [ADR-0004](../notes/ADRs.md) (2026-07-23): one artifact, one source
  tree, organized by capability (`corpus.*`, `gate.*`, `artifact`, `cli`),
  not by format or tool.
- **Distribution coordinates** (open as of 2026-07-23): Clojars vs. Maven
  Central. Decided at first release, not before — there's nothing to
  publish yet and no reason to lock in coordinates early.

========== END FILE ==========

========== FILE: ./docs/research/EHR-testing-tools-selection-research.md ==========
# Component Selection Research for an Open-Source EHR Testing-Tools Repository

**Scope:** Synthetic longitudinal patient generation (HL7 v2 + FHIR, C-CDA later), controlled labelled mutation, structural + profile validation gates, and profile authoring/versioning — usable in-process or as a subprocess from Clojure/JVM, locally deployable, offline after one-time artifact acquisition, deterministic, free/OSS with transitive-license compatibility.

**Checked date:** 2026-07-22. Confidence markers used per keyed question: **Verified** (read the code/schema/test/issue that states it), **Reported** (stated by an authoritative primary doc but not code-verified), **Inferred** (deduced from cited evidence), **Unknown**. ⚠ marks load-bearing items requiring code/schema/test evidence or an explicit settling experiment. **Methods/limitations:** see final section.

---

## Executive Summary

**Strongest candidate per capability, with the single decisive fact (all dated to fetched sources):**

- **FHIR generation — Synthea** (Apache-2.0, MITRE): the only mature seeded JVM generator; native FHIR **R4/STU3/DSTU2**, C-CDA, CSV, CPCDS, **no R4B/R5, no HL7v2** ([Synthea README, fetched 2026-07-22](https://github.com/synthetichealth/synthea); [export source dir](https://api.github.com/repos/synthetichealth/synthea/contents/src/main/java/org/mitre/synthea/export)). Latest tagged release **v4.0.0, published 2026-03-05** ([Synthea releases API](https://api.github.com/repos/synthetichealth/synthea/releases?per_page=2)).
- **HL7 v2 generation — none adopt-ready**: Synthea has no mainline v2 exporter; the only v2 code is an unmerged 2021 PR its own author calls "a bit of a hack… not suitable for inclusion" ([PR #862](https://github.com/synthetichealth/synthea/pull/862)). Recommended path: a purpose-built `PatientExporter` plugin ([Custom Exporters wiki](https://github.com/synthetichealth/synthea/wiki/Custom-Exporters)) projecting to v2 via **HAPI HL7v2** ([Maven Central `ca.uhn.hapi:hapi-base`](https://search.maven.org/solrsearch/select?q=g:ca.uhn.hapi+AND+a:hapi-base&rows=3&wt=json)).
- **FHIR validation — official validator `org.hl7.fhir.core` / `validator_cli`** (Apache-2.0, HL7-maintained), latest **6.9.12 published 2026-07-15** ([release 6.9.12](https://github.com/hapifhir/org.hl7.fhir.core/releases/tag/6.9.12)). HAPI FHIR's validator is the **same engine** wrapped differently ([HAPI instance validator docs](https://hapifhir.io/hapi-fhir/docs/validation/instance_validator.html)).
- **HL7 v2 profile validation — CDC `lib-hl7v2-nist-validator`** (Apache-2.0) wrapping the **NIST `v2-validation`** Scala engine, which is the only surveyed engine that enforces predicates/co-constraints, verified in engine source ([NIST content `DefaultValidator.scala`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/usnistgov/v2-validation/master/validation/src/main/scala/hl7/v2/validation/content/DefaultValidator.scala)). HAPI HL7v2's in-process `DefaultValidator` enforces usage/cardinality/length/table only ([HAPI `DefaultValidator.java`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/hapifhir/hapi-hl7v2/master/hapi-base/src/main/java/ca/uhn/hl7v2/conf/check/DefaultValidator.java)).
- **Profile authoring — FSH + SUSHI** (Apache-2.0, Node.js 18, not JVM) for FHIR ([SUSHI README](https://github.com/FHIR/sushi)); **IGAMT** (Spring Boot + MongoDB + Angular) for HL7 v2 conformance profiles ([hl7-igamt pom.xml](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/usnistgov/hl7-igamt/master/pom.xml)).
- **Mutation — none adopt-ready** in surveyed repositories; build in-house (see B1 for scope of search).

**Three findings that most shape the design:** (1) the official FHIR validator is **not cleanly offline** — even with locally packaged ValueSets, `-tx n/a` produces different, failing results, an **open** bug with no fix as of 2026-07-22 ([issue #2272, state open](https://github.com/hapifhir/org.hl7.fhir.core/issues/2272)); (2) it has a **live, non-deterministic concurrency bug** in `ProfileUtilities.fixTypeOfResourceId()`, **open**, unpatched through 6.9.9 ([issue #2470, state open](https://github.com/hapifhir/org.hl7.fhir.core/issues/2470)); (3) the IGAMT export bundle and HAPI's `message_profile.xsd` are **different profile dialects** ([HAPI `ProfileParser.java`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/hapifhir/hapi-hl7v2/master/hapi-base/src/main/java/ca/uhn/hl7v2/conf/parser/ProfileParser.java); [CDC lib README](https://github.com/CDCgov/lib-hl7v2-nist-validator)), so IGAMT profiles route to the NIST/CDC engine, not HAPI.

**License spine:** Apache-2.0 for Synthea, official validator, HAPI FHIR, SUSHI, Inferno, CDC wrapper. **HAPI HL7v2 is dual MPL/GPL with a user *choice* of either license — GPL is not mandatory** ([HAPI HL7v2 pom.xml license block](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/hapifhir/hapi-hl7v2/master/pom.xml)). NIST `v2-validation` and IGAMT ship **no LICENSE file** (resolved below as far as primary sources allow). Terminologies split sharply: LOINC and UCUM are royalty-free-redistributable with notice; **SNOMED CT and CPT require licenses**; ICD-10-CM is distributed free by CMS/CDC.

**Questions resolvable only by experiment (defined in Proposed Experiments):** Synthea byte/order determinism across thread counts (A4); HAPI FHIR parser round-trip fidelity (B2); OperationOutcome code/location stability across pinned versions (C3); minimal freely-redistributable offline terminology set (C5); IGAMT-`PROFILE.xml`-vs-HAPI-XSD compatibility (D1); CDC-lib fully-offline build (D3); and startup/memory/throughput for every candidate (E6). *No final architecture is recommended; options are ranked with tradeoffs and kept separable from findings.*

---

## Findings by Question

### A. Synthetic Generation

#### A1 — Generators, native formats and versions (survey scope stated)
**Answer:** Synthea is the only mature seeded longitudinal JVM generator. Native outputs: **HL7 FHIR R4, STU3 (3.0.1), DSTU2 (1.0.2); Bulk FHIR ndjson; C-CDA; CSV; CPCDS** — **no FHIR R4B/R5 and no HL7v2**. **Search scope for alternatives:** GitHub repository/topic search and org-scoped queries for FHIR/HL7v2 generators and converters (`synthetichealth`, `LinuxForHealth`, `microsoft`, `nextgenhealthcare`, `usnistgov`), plus general web search on synthetic-patient and FHIR-tooling indexes. Surfaced alternatives and their constraints: **`FHIRTestResourceGenerator`** (Java, single-resource randomizer, not longitudinal, license NOASSERTION); **Inferno reference generators** (Ruby, non-JVM); **Crucible** (archived); hosted/proprietary EHR sandboxes (not local). No maintained JVM generator with native HL7v2 output was found within this scope.
**Evidence:** format table in [Synthea README](https://github.com/synthetichealth/synthea); exporter classes (`FhirR4/FhirStu3/FhirDstu2`, `CCDAExporter`, `CPCDSExporter`, `CSVExporter`, no v2 file) in [export source dir](https://api.github.com/repos/synthetichealth/synthea/contents/src/main/java/org/mitre/synthea/export); [FHIRTestResourceGenerator](https://github.com/geloro94/FHIRTestResourceGenerator) (Java, single-resource); [Crucible archived listing](https://ecqi.healthit.gov/tool/crucible).
**Version/date:** Synthea README + release v4.0.0 (2026-03-05); fetched 2026-07-22.
**Confidence:** Formats/versions **Verified**; universal-absence claim bounded to the stated search scope → **Inferred**.

#### A2 ⚠ — Synthea HL7v2 exporter (versions/types/segments/quality/maintenance)
**Answer:** **No HL7v2 exporter exists in mainline Synthea.** The only v2 code is unmerged **PR #862** (2021): `HL7V2Exporter.java` (~626 lines) emitting "fat" **ADT** messages via **HAPI HL7v2 2.5.1**, with `MSH/PID/PD1/IN1/SFT` plus meds/labs/vitals and custom Z-segments, gated on `exporter.hl7v2.export`. **Closed by its own author** as "too use-case specific… a bit of a kludge… not suitable for inclusion." Feature request **#1561 (opened 2025-02-12) is open with no maintainer commitment**. The endorsed forward path is a `PatientExporter` ServiceLoader plugin.
**Evidence (PR/issue/code, per ⚠):** [PR #862, state Closed](https://github.com/synthetichealth/synthea/pull/862) — adds `HL7V2Exporter.java (626)`, "Change HAPI to 2.5.1", "Added a minimal IN1, SFT and PD1 segment"; author (2024-08-03) "was a bit of a hack and not suitable for inclusion"; maintainer cites `PatientExporter.export(Person, long, ExporterRuntimeOptions)`. [Issue #1561, state open](https://github.com/synthetichealth/synthea/issues/1561).
**Confidence:** **Verified.**

#### A3 — FHIR→v2 converters, standalone v2 generators, template methods, minimal projection (expanded)
**Answer:** Direct FHIR→HL7v2 conversion tooling is scarce and constrained:
- **Microsoft `FHIR-Converter`** (MIT): supports **FHIR→HL7v2 (Preview)** plus HL7v2/C-CDA→FHIR, via **Liquid templates on the .NET runtime — not JVM** ([FHIR-Converter README](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/microsoft/FHIR-Converter/main/README.md); [repo/license MIT](https://github.com/microsoft/FHIR-Converter)). Active (pushed 2026-06-03).
- **Mirth Connect / NextGen `connect`** (**MPL 2.0**, single copyleft, **Java**): channel + template (JavaScript/velocity) transformation engine that can map FHIR↔HL7v2; general-purpose integration engine, not a purpose-built projector ([Mirth LICENSE = Mozilla Public License 2.0](https://github.com/nextgenhealthcare/connect/blob/master/LICENSE)). Last push 2024-09-23 ([repo metadata](https://api.github.com/repos/nextgenhealthcare/connect)).
- **LinuxForHealth `hl7v2-fhir-converter`** (active, pushed 2026-02-11) converts **v2→FHIR only** (wrong direction) ([LinuxForHealth repository](https://github.com/LinuxForHealth/hl7v2-fhir-converter)).
- **Caristix / HL7-Soup / Message Workbench:** commercial or Windows-desktop tooling; not evaluated as OSS/JVM libraries within scope (no maintained OSS JVM library surfaced).
Building blocks for a JVM projection: **HAPI HL7v2** typed structures/encoders (Maven Central) and **Synthea's in-repo `flexporter`** FHIRPath mapping engine. **Minimal purpose-built projection estimate:** a Synthea `PatientExporter` plugin emitting ADT^A01/A04 (`MSH/EVN/PID/PV1`, optionally `AL1/DG1/PR1/OBX/IN1`) — small-to-medium effort by analogy to the 626-line 2021 PR.
**Evidence:** direction/runtime facts cited above; `flexporter` files (`Actions.java`, `CustomFHIRPathResourceGeneratorR4.java`, `Mapping.java`) in [flexporter source dir](https://api.github.com/repos/synthetichealth/synthea/contents/src/main/java/org/mitre/synthea/export/flexporter); plugin interface in [Custom Exporters wiki](https://github.com/synthetichealth/synthea/wiki/Custom-Exporters).
**Confidence:** Converter capabilities/licenses/directions **Verified**; effort estimate **Inferred**.

#### A4 ⚠ — Byte determinism; complete pinned-input list; nondeterminism evidence
**Answer:** Populations are **reproducible per (seed + Synthea version + config)**; thread count does **not** change *which* patients are produced because each person's seed is drawn sequentially from the population RNG before thread submission. **Byte-determinism additionally requires pinning:** `-s` (population seed), `-cs` (clinician seed), `-r` (reference date) — all default to `System.currentTimeMillis()` — plus the Synthea **version**, module set (`-d`), config (`-c`), locale/geography, and `generate.thread_pool_size` (defaults to `availableProcessors()`). Output-file *ordering* across differing thread counts is **Unknown** (experiment EXP-A4).
**Evidence (code, per ⚠):** `seed = referenceTime`, `clinicianSeed = referenceTime`, `referenceTime = System.currentTimeMillis()`; `threadPoolSize = Config.getAsInteger("generate.thread_pool_size", -1)` → `availableProcessors()`; per-person seed `final long seed = this.populationRandom.randLong(); threadPool.submit(() -> generatePerson(index, seed));` in [Generator.java](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/synthetichealth/synthea/master/src/main/java/org/mitre/synthea/engine/Generator.java). Vendor identity claim "populations generated with the same seed and the same version of Synthea should be identical" in [Basic Setup wiki](https://github.com/synthetichealth/synthea/wiki/Basic-Setup-and-Running).
**Confidence:** Seed/thread independence and required pins **Verified**; exact byte-identical file ordering across thread counts **Unknown** (EXP-A4).

#### A5 — Network fetches / offline disablement
**Answer:** Synthea runs fully locally from the fat JAR; geography/module data ship in resources, and the US Core IG flag selects bundled profiles rather than downloading. No mandatory runtime network fetch was found in the run path read.
**Evidence:** local-only `java -jar` invocations in [Basic Setup wiki](https://github.com/synthetichealth/synthea/wiki/Basic-Setup-and-Running); bundled MA/US data in [Common Configuration wiki](https://github.com/synthetichealth/synthea/wiki/Common-Configuration); only console prints (no network) in the read section of [Generator.java](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/synthetichealth/synthea/master/src/main/java/org/mitre/synthea/engine/Generator.java).
**Confidence:** **Inferred** (not exhaustively audited across every exporter).

#### A6 — JVM library/subprocess (functional integration only; resource profile in E6)
**Answer:** Usable in-process (`Generator`) or as a subprocess (`java -jar synthea-with-dependencies.jar …`); requires **JDK 11+ (LTS 11/17 recommended)**; multi-threaded fixed pool sized to CPU count. Concrete startup/memory/throughput → see **E6** (Unknown; benchmark EXP-E6).
**Evidence:** JDK requirement in [Synthea README](https://github.com/synthetichealth/synthea); threading in [Generator.java](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/synthetichealth/synthea/master/src/main/java/org/mitre/synthea/engine/Generator.java).
**Confidence:** JVM/subprocess/Java version **Verified**; performance **Unknown** (E6).

#### A7 — Custom modules/exporters and learning curve
**Answer:** Two seams: JSON disease/care modules and custom exporters (`PatientExporter` per-patient, `PostCompletionExporter` once) loaded as drop-in JARs via Java **ServiceLoader** (`META-INF/services/…`), documented as "fairly simple," requiring JVM code (Kotlin/Scala possible).
**Evidence:** interfaces, `./gradlew jar`, `-cp`/`./lib/custom/`, example project in [Custom Exporters wiki](https://github.com/synthetichealth/synthea/wiki/Custom-Exporters).
**Confidence:** **Verified.**

#### A8 — Directly controllable characteristics vs filtering/seed-search
**Answer:** Direct controls: population size (`-p`), gender (`-g`), age range (`-a`), geography. Finer targeting is **filter/retry-based** (`checkCriteria` + `maxAttemptsToKeepPatient` re-roll loop), i.e., seed-search, not direct synthesis.
**Evidence:** flags in [Basic Setup wiki](https://github.com/synthetichealth/synthea/wiki/Basic-Setup-and-Running); retry loop `do{…createPerson…checkCriteria…}while(!patientMeetsCriteria)` in [Generator.java](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/synthetichealth/synthea/master/src/main/java/org/mitre/synthea/engine/Generator.java).
**Confidence:** **Verified.**

---

### B. Mutation

#### B1 — Open-source controlled/labelled mutators (search scope stated)
**Answer:** **No maintained open-source mutator emitting FHIR/HL7v2 defects labelled with (operator, location, violated constraint) was found in the surveyed repositories/indexes.** **Search scope:** GitHub repository/topic/code search for "FHIR mutation", "HL7 fuzzer", "structure-aware fuzzing", healthcare test-data mutation; general web search across FHIR-tooling indexes and HL7 security materials. Found: **`IOActive/HL7-Fuzzer`** (MIT, **Python**, security-oriented, unlabelled, last push 2021-01-13, [GitHub repository metadata](https://api.github.com/repos/IOActive/HL7-Fuzzer)); generic mutators **Radamsa** and libFuzzer/`Arbitrary` (cited in [HL7 "Fuzzing with FHIR" materials](https://info.hl7.org/hubfs/FHIR%20Open%20Source%20Tooling%20Webinar%20Series/PDFs/FHIR%20Security%20Education%20Event%20-%20Fuzzing%20with%20FHIR%20-%20FINAL%20Brett%20P%20Stringham-V3.1%20Brett%20Stringham.pdf)); and **`FHIRTestResourceGenerator`** (Java, controlled FHIRPath-map modification, no defect taxonomy, [FHIRTestResourceGenerator repository](https://github.com/geloro94/FHIRTestResourceGenerator)).
**Confidence:** Verified-absence **bounded to the stated scope** → **Inferred** for the universal claim.

#### B2 ⚠ — Round-trip fidelity (HAPI HL7v2 code-verified; HAPI FHIR by experiment)
**Answer:** **HAPI HL7v2 `PipeParser` round-trip is canonicalizing, not byte-exact:** on encode it **strips trailing/extra delimiters** at field and segment level, dropping trailing empty fields; a `forceUpToFieldNum` option can re-pad to a minimum field count. Mutation harnesses must therefore mutate the encoded string or verify post-encode. **HAPI FHIR JSON/XML parser fidelity is Unknown** (not code-verified) → EXP-B2.
**Evidence (code, per ⚠):** `stripExtraDelimiters(...)` "search from back end for first occurrence of non-delimiter" and "strip trailing delimiters …" with `forceUpToFieldNum` re-padding in [PipeParser.java](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/hapifhir/hapi-hl7v2/master/hapi-base/src/main/java/ca/uhn/hl7v2/parser/PipeParser.java). HAPI FHIR parsers "very lightweight … do not need to be reused" in [HAPI intro docs](https://hapifhir.io/hapi-fhir/docs/getting_started/introduction.html).
**Confidence:** HL7v2 canonicalization **Verified**; HAPI FHIR byte-fidelity **Unknown** (EXP-B2).

#### B3 — Healthcare defect taxonomies; generic vs format-specific (broadened)
**Answer:** No canonical healthcare *mutation* taxonomy found; the operative error taxonomies to mutate against are: FHIR **OperationOutcome issue codes** (`code-invalid`, `structure`, `invalid`, `required`, …); HL7 v2 HAPI exception classes (`ProfileNotFollowedException`, `ProfileNotHL7CompliantException`, `XElementPresentException`) and NIST report classifications (**ERROR/WARNING/ALERT/AFFIRMATION**); C-CDA schema + Schematron rule IDs. Broader mutation-testing prior art (generic, not healthcare-specific) includes property-based generators and mutation-testing frameworks (e.g., PIT/mutation operators), which supply operator vocabularies (replace/insert/delete/boundary) reusable as mutation-operator labels.
**Evidence:** FHIR codes/expressions in [issue #2272](https://github.com/hapifhir/org.hl7.fhir.core/issues/2272); HAPI v2 exception types in [conf/check dir](https://api.github.com/repos/hapifhir/hapi-hl7v2/contents/hapi-base/src/main/java/ca/uhn/hl7v2/conf/check); NIST classifications ERROR/WARNING/ALERT/AFFIRMATION in [CDC lib README](https://github.com/CDCgov/lib-hl7v2-nist-validator); C-CDA schema+Schematron in [C-CDA companion guide](https://build.fhir.org/ig/HL7/CDA-ccda-2.1-sd/understanding_c-cda_and_the_c-cda_companion_guide.html).
**Confidence:** Operative error taxonomies **Verified**; dedicated mutation taxonomy **Verified absence** (scoped).

#### B4 — Mutation-provenance schemas/conventions (broadened)
**Answer:** No healthcare-specific mutation-provenance schema found. Reusable location grammars for an (operator, location, violated-constraint) label: **FHIRPath** expressions (used by the validator's OperationOutcome `expression` and by `FHIRTestResourceGenerator`'s FHIRPath→value map) for FHIR; **HL7 v2 Terser paths** (`GROUP/SEGMENT-field(rep)-component-subcomponent`) for v2. Generic provenance conventions from mutation testing (mutant ID + operator + target location + kill status) map cleanly onto these.
**Evidence:** OperationOutcome `expression` FHIRPath locations in [issue #2272](https://github.com/hapifhir/org.hl7.fhir.core/issues/2272); FHIRPath-map approach in [FHIRTestResourceGenerator](https://github.com/geloro94/FHIRTestResourceGenerator); Terser paths (`currentTerserPath`) in [PipeParser.java](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/hapifhir/hapi-hl7v2/master/hapi-base/src/main/java/ca/uhn/hl7v2/parser/PipeParser.java).
**Confidence:** **Inferred** (no dedicated schema; natural conventions identified).

---

### C. FHIR Validation

#### C1 ⚠ — Exact current offline procedure, cache locations, air-gapped invocation, cache bugs
**Answer:** Two **distinct caches** are involved:
- **Package cache** (FHIR IG NPM tarballs). Default locations, verified from `FilesystemPackageCacheManager` source: user cache `${user.home}/.fhir/packages`; system cache Windows `${ProgramData}/.fhir/packages`, else `/var/lib/.fhir/packages`; temp `[tmp]/.fhir/packages`; overridable via a builder `withCacheFolder(path)`. Default package servers are `packages.fhir.org`/`packages2.fhir.org`, and **default servers can be disabled** via `FhirSettings.isIgnoreDefaultPackageServers()` (the air-gap knob), with network access routed through `ManagedWebAccess`.
- **Terminology cache** (tx server responses), a separate location surfaced in the log and set by `-txcache <dir>`, cleared with `-clear-tx-cache`, or disabled with `-txcache n/a`.

**One-time acquisition (online):** fetch `validator_cli.jar` from GitHub releases (6.9.12) or Maven `ca.uhn.hapi.fhir:org.hl7.fhir.validation.cli`; pre-load each IG and its transitive dependencies (pinned `id#version`) into `${user.home}/.fhir/packages`; optionally warm the terminology cache.
**Fully air-gapped invocation:** disable default package servers (FhirSettings), then `java -jar validator_cli.jar -version 4.0.1 -ig hl7.fhir.us.core#<ver> -tx n/a -txcache <local-dir> src.json` reading only local caches.
**Known offline/version-skew/cache bugs (status as of 2026-07-22):** (a) **#2272** — `-tx n/a` still fails ValueSet-membership/slice/display checks even for package-local ValueSets: **open, no fixed version found**; (b) **#2524** — `TerminologyCache` persists transient tx failures, poisoning the on-disk cache: **open, no fixed version found**; (c) **#2462** — locale-sensitive behavior can break local builds on non-English JVM locales: **open, no fixed version found**; (d) a proxy-for-HTTPS CLI issue was **closed** (unrelated to air-gap). No issue establishing a fixed version for the offline-terminology coupling was found.
**Evidence (per ⚠):** cache paths `${user.home}/.fhir/packages`, `${ProgramData}/.fhir/packages`, `/var/lib/.fhir/packages`, `[tmp]/.fhir/packages`, `withCacheFolder`, `isIgnoreDefaultPackageServers`, `ManagedWebAccess` in [FilesystemPackageCacheManager.java](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/hapifhir/org.hl7.fhir.core/master/org.hl7.fhir.utilities/src/main/java/org/hl7/fhir/utilities/npm/FilesystemPackageCacheManager.java); `-tx n/a`, `-txcache`, `-clear-tx-cache`, `-ig id#version` in [Using the FHIR Validator (HL7 confluence)](https://confluence.hl7.org/spaces/FHIR/pages/35718580/Using+the+FHIR+Validator); tarball registry + pinned versions in [FHIR packages spec](https://hl7.org/fhir/packages.html); [release 6.9.12 (2026-07-15)](https://github.com/hapifhir/org.hl7.fhir.core/releases/tag/6.9.12); bug states [#2272 open](https://github.com/hapifhir/org.hl7.fhir.core/issues/2272), [#2524 open](https://github.com/hapifhir/org.hl7.fhir.core/issues/2524), [#2462 open](https://github.com/hapifhir/org.hl7.fhir.core/issues/2462).
**Confidence:** Cache paths/flags/air-gap knob **Verified**; residual offline failures **Verified** (open bugs); minimal terminology that yields a clean offline pass **Unknown** (EXP-C5).

#### C2 — Library vs subprocess; Java, global state, API stability (resource profile in E6)
**Answer:** Both entry points exist. **Subprocess** isolates global state/version — recommended for determinism/CI. **Library** (Maven `org.hl7.fhir.validation.cli`) avoids process spawn but exposes shared mutable caches (see #2470) and requires wiring package/terminology support in code. **Java 11+ (JDK 11/17 tested).** Validation-support interfaces were reworked in HAPI 5.x (`IContextValidationSupport`→`IValidationSupport`). Memory/startup → **E6**.
**Evidence:** library/CLI + Maven coords + Java 11 min in [org.hl7.fhir.core README](https://github.com/hapifhir/org.hl7.fhir.core); JDK 11/17 tested in [confluence](https://confluence.hl7.org/spaces/FHIR/pages/35718580/Using+the+FHIR+Validator); concurrency/global-state hazard in [issue #2470](https://github.com/hapifhir/org.hl7.fhir.core/issues/2470); API rework in [HAPI instance validator docs](https://hapifhir.io/hapi-fhir/docs/validation/instance_validator.html).
**Confidence:** **Verified.**

#### C3 ⚠ — OperationOutcome stability across versions
**Answer:** Message **text is not stable** across releases (release notes routinely reword messages), and terminology configuration changes both which issues appear and their `code`. `expression`/`code` are more stable than `details.text` but not guaranteed. **CI gates should assert on `{severity, code, expression}` with pinned validator+IG+terminology.** A full code/location stability matrix requires diffing two pinned versions → EXP-C3.
**Evidence (per ⚠):** "improve validation error message for retired codes" (text change) in [release 6.9.12 notes](https://github.com/hapifhir/org.hl7.fhir.core/releases/tag/6.9.12); version-dependent `code`/`details.text`/`expression` payloads in [issue #2272](https://github.com/hapifhir/org.hl7.fhir.core/issues/2272).
**Confidence:** Text-instability **Verified**; full stability matrix **Reported → EXP-C3**.

#### C4 — FSH→SUSHI→package authoring; alternatives; offline
**Answer:** **FSH + SUSHI** compiles FHIR Shorthand to profile/IG output for **R4/R4B/R5**; **requires Node.js 18 (not JVM)**; Apache-2.0. It does only **basic** validation (paths, cardinalities) — not FHIRPath, terminology, or slice-membership — so profiles still need the official validator. Offline after setup is plausible but `update-dependencies`/IG Publisher fetch packages, so **one-time online setup is required**. Alternatives: Trifolia-on-FHIR (JS/Angular + Java IG Publisher); Firely Forge (proprietary).
**Evidence:** Node.js 18, Apache-2.0, R4/R4B/R5, "basic validation … does not validate … terminological compliance, or slice membership," `update-dependencies` in [SUSHI README](https://github.com/FHIR/sushi); Trifolia-on-FHIR stack in [Lantana free tools](https://www.lantanagroup.com/resources/free-tools/).
**Confidence:** Node dependency + capabilities **Verified**; full offline-after-setup **Inferred**.

#### C5 ⚠ — `-tx n/a` behavior (performed/emitted/skipped) + terminology licenses (primary sources)
**Answer, validator behavior under `-tx n/a`** (precise trichotomy):
- **Performed:** structural conformance, cardinality, datatype, fixed/pattern values, profile-shape, and any invariant/FHIRPath checks not requiring terminology.
- **Errors emitted (false negatives offline):** ValueSet-membership checks against required bindings can **fail** with `code-invalid` even when the ValueSet is package-local; profile slices whose discrimination depends on terminology can **fail** with a `structure` "matching slice … not found" error; changed display names can **emit** a display-validation `invalid` error.
- **Skipped/indeterminate:** where no local definition exists, the validator reports a **warning/indeterminate** (e.g., "A definition for CodeSystem … could not be found, so the code cannot be validated") rather than pass/fail.

**Terminology licenses (each from its primary source):**
| Terminology | Redistributable in local fixtures? | Primary terms |
|---|---|---|
| **LOINC** | Yes, royalty-free, with notice/version conditions | "granted in perpetuity, without payment of license fees or royalties, to use, copy, or distribute" ([LOINC license](https://loinc.org/license/)) |
| **UCUM** | Yes, royalty-free **if unmodified** (no derivative works; attribution) | "no-charge, royalty-free … reproduce … and distribute the Work," with "may not … create Derivative Works" ([UCUM license](https://unitsofmeasure.org/license)) |
| **SNOMED CT** | No, requires an Affiliate License (free in Member countries with registration; fees in non-Member territories) | "does not charge … in … Member countries"; "required to apply for a license through … MLDS … annual basis" for non-Member ([SNOMED International Get SNOMED](https://www.snomed.org/get-snomed)) |
| **CPT** | No, proprietary AMA code set requiring license/royalties | AMA "CPT licensing FAQs" and "Royalties Portal" gate access ([AMA CPT page](https://www.ama-assn.org/practice-management/cpt)) |
| **ICD-10-CM** | Yes (US), distributed free by CMS/CDC; no license/fee stated | CMS "ICD-10 applies to all parties covered by HIPAA"; no cost/license stated on the file page ([CMS ICD-10](https://www.cms.gov/medicare/coding-billing/icd-10-codes)) |
| **HL7 Terminology (THO) / HL7 code systems** | Presumed free (HL7); not confirmed on fetched page | THO license page did not render terms; US Core package declares `"license": "CC0-1.0"` in the [FHIR packages spec example](https://hl7.org/fhir/packages.html), and the [THO license page](https://terminology.hl7.org/license.html) did not state the license text — **Unknown from fetched source**. |

**On a "local terminology server":** do **not** assume a drop-in offline `tx.fhir.org`. A locally deployable OSS terminology server should be independently selected and license-cleared before relying on it; this report does not certify one. The safest offline path is to bundle only the royalty-free vocabularies (LOINC, UCUM, ICD-10-CM, HL7/CC0) as package CodeSystem/ValueSet resources and accept indeterminate results for SNOMED/CPT-bound checks (EXP-C5).
**Evidence (per ⚠):** trichotomy and exact messages/codes in [issue #2272](https://github.com/hapifhir/org.hl7.fhir.core/issues/2272); `-tx n/a` semantics in [confluence](https://confluence.hl7.org/spaces/FHIR/pages/35718580/Using+the+FHIR+Validator); license rows cited inline above.
**Confidence:** Behavior trichotomy **Verified**; LOINC/UCUM/SNOMED/CPT/ICD-10-CM terms **Verified**; THO exact license **Unknown**; minimal offline set **Unknown** (EXP-C5).

#### C6 — HAPI FHIR validator vs official validator: divergence and authority
**Answer:** Effectively the **same engine**: HAPI FHIR's "reference validator" (`FhirInstanceValidator` + `ValidationSupportChain`) delegates to `org.hl7.fhir.core`, both maintained under `ca.uhn.hapi.fhir`. **Authority = `org.hl7.fhir.core`/`validator_cli`.** Divergence stems from validation-support configuration (terminology-support modules, package loading), not a different rule engine.
**Evidence:** `ValidationSupportChain`, `DefaultProfileValidationSupport`, `InMemoryTerminologyServerValidationSupport`, `NpmPackageValidationSupport` in [HAPI instance validator docs](https://hapifhir.io/hapi-fhir/docs/validation/instance_validator.html); shared core in [org.hl7.fhir.core README](https://github.com/hapifhir/org.hl7.fhir.core).
**Confidence:** **Verified.**

---

### D. HL7 v2 Profile Validation

#### D1 ⚠ — Highest-priority handshake: IGAMT export vs HAPI ProfileParser
**Answer:** **Not directly compatible; use the NIST/CDC path for IGAMT profiles, or translate.** HAPI's `ProfileParser` validates against its own XSD `ca/uhn/hl7v2/conf/parser/message_profile.xsd`, root **`HL7v2xConformanceProfile`**, building `RuntimeProfile`/`StaticDef` ("HL7 version 2.5 section 2.12"). IGAMT exports a **multi-file bundle** (`PROFILE.xml`, `CONSTRAINTS.xml`, `VALUESETS.xml`, `VALUESETBINDINGS.xml`, `COCONSTRAINTS.xml`, `SLICINGS.xml`) built for the NIST engine — a different dialect. Exact compatibility of a real IGAMT `PROFILE.xml` with HAPI's XSD is settled only by EXP-D1.
**Evidence (schema/code, per ⚠):** `PROFILE_XSD = "ca/uhn/hl7v2/conf/parser/message_profile.xsd"`, `parseStaticProfile`, "RuntimeProfile … HL7 version 2.5 section 2.12" in [ProfileParser.java](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/hapifhir/hapi-hl7v2/master/hapi-base/src/main/java/ca/uhn/hl7v2/conf/parser/ProfileParser.java); root `HL7v2xConformanceProfile` + attributes `Usage/Min/Max/Length/Datatype/Table/Predicate/Reference` in [message_profile.xsd](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/hapifhir/hapi-hl7v2/master/hapi-base/src/main/resources/ca/uhn/hl7v2/conf/parser/message_profile.xsd); IGAMT export file set in [CDC lib README](https://github.com/CDCgov/lib-hl7v2-nist-validator).
**Confidence:** Dialect divergence **Verified**; exact incompatibility of a real export **Reported → EXP-D1**.

#### D2 ⚠ — HAPI constraints actually enforced
**Answer:** HAPI `DefaultValidator` enforces **Usage** (R/RE/X/O; `X` skipped), **Cardinality** (min/max reps), **Length** (max), **Datatype/structure** (recurses fields/components/subcomponents), and **Table membership** (only for `IS`/`ID`/`CE` types, and only if a `CodeStore` is registered — otherwise silently skipped). It does **NOT** enforce **predicates/conditional usage** (explicit code comment "can't test anything yet -- wait for condition syntax in v2.6") or **co-constraints**.
**Evidence (code, per ⚠):** `testUsage`, `testCardinality`, `checkLength`/`testLength`, `testTypeAgainstTable` (IS/ID/CE only) + `testValueAgainstTable` (skips with "no code store"/"Don't have a table"), predicate comment "can't test anything yet -- wait for condition syntax in v2.6" in [DefaultValidator.java](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/hapifhir/hapi-hl7v2/master/hapi-base/src/main/java/ca/uhn/hl7v2/conf/check/DefaultValidator.java).
**Confidence:** **Verified.**

#### D3 ⚠ — CDC lib-hl7v2-nist-validator: target, inputs, API, license, maintenance, transitives, Maven, offline
**Answer:** **Wraps** the NIST `v2-validation` engine. **Inputs:** IGAMT-exported XML per profile folder (`PROFILE.xml` required; `CONSTRAINTS.xml`, `VALUESETS.xml`, `VALUESETBINDINGS.xml`, `COCONSTRAINTS.xml`, `SLICINGS.xml` optional; exact case-sensitive names). **API:** `ProfileManager(ProfileFetcher, profileName).validate(message) → NistReport` (filtered ERROR/WARNING); a `ResourceFileFetcher` reads profiles from resources. **License: Apache-2.0** (LICENSE text is verbatim Apache 2.0). **Maintenance:** latest release **1.4.0 published 2025-08-29** ([releases API](https://api.github.com/repos/CDCgov/lib-hl7v2-nist-validator/releases)); uses NIST lib **1.6.3**. **Transitives/Maven: NOT on Maven Central** — NIST jars come from **NIST Nexus** (`hit-nexus.nist.gov`) via Maven flags disabling SSL verification. **Offline:** works from local resources once NIST jars are cached; the initial dependency fetch is a special-case network step (EXP-D3 verifies fully-offline).
**Evidence (per ⚠):** wrap target, input file set, `ProfileManager`/`ProfileFetcher`/`NistReport`, Nexus URL + `-Dmaven.wagon.http.ssl.insecure=true`, NIST lib 1.6.3 in [CDC lib README](https://github.com/CDCgov/lib-hl7v2-nist-validator); Apache-2.0 text in [CDC LICENSE](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CDCgov/lib-hl7v2-nist-validator/main/LICENSE); release 1.4.0 (2025-08-29) in [releases API](https://api.github.com/repos/CDCgov/lib-hl7v2-nist-validator/releases).
**Confidence:** **Verified** (target/inputs/API/license/Maven-absence/latest release); fully-offline build **Reported → EXP-D3**.

#### D4 ⚠ — NIST engine: embeddable JVM, license, constraints beyond HAPI (verified from engine source)
**Answer:** `usnistgov/v2-validation` is a **Scala JVM library**; its validation is organized into `structure`, `content`, and `vs` (value-set) modules. **Enforcement verified from source:** the `content/DefaultValidator.scala` `checkContent` path retrieves and evaluates `Constraint` (conformance statements via `constraintsFor`), `Predicate` (`predicatesFor`), and `CoConstraint` (`coConstraintsFor`, with `checkCC` emitting `coConstraintSuccess`/`coConstraintFailure`); `structure` enforces usage/cardinality/length/datatype; `vs` handles value-set validation. This is **strictly more than HAPI's DefaultValidator** (predicates + co-constraints). Dependencies are NIST-hosted jars (`gov.nist:xml-util`, `com.github.hl7-tools:validation-report`), not Maven Central. **License: no LICENSE file and no source-header license text found → Unknown** (as a US Government (NIST) work it is likely public-domain/US-Gov, but this is not stated in the fetched sources; resolve via EXP-SBOM).
**Evidence (engine source, per ⚠):** `constraintsFor(e)`, `predicatesFor(e)`, `coConstraintsFor(e)`, `checkCC`, `coConstraintSuccess/Failure` in [content/DefaultValidator.scala](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/usnistgov/v2-validation/master/validation/src/main/scala/hl7/v2/validation/content/DefaultValidator.scala); `CoConstraint`/`PlainCoConstraint`/`Constraint` case classes in [content/Constraint.scala](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/usnistgov/v2-validation/master/validation/src/main/scala/hl7/v2/validation/content/Constraint.scala); module layout (`structure`, `content`, `vs`) in [validation source dir](https://api.github.com/repos/usnistgov/v2-validation/contents/validation/src/main/scala/hl7/v2/validation); NIST-hosted deps in [NIST v2-validation README](https://github.com/usnistgov/v2-validation); no LICENSE in [repo contents](https://api.github.com/repos/usnistgov/v2-validation/contents/).
**Confidence:** Constraint superset (predicates/co-constraints) **Verified from source**; license **Unknown**.

#### D5 — IGAMT self-hosting: license, stack, releases (verified as far as sources allow)
**Answer:** IGAMT is a heavyweight **Spring Boot + MongoDB + Redis + Spring Security** Java backend (multi-module Maven, modules incl. `conformance-profile`, `co-constraint`, `constraint`, `valueset`, `export`, `verification`) with two **Angular clients** (`igamt-hl7-client-v1`, `igamt-hl7-client-v2`) and a `Dockerfile`; self-hosting is a full service deployment, not a library. **Activity:** default branch `master`, latest commit "Release 2.11" dated **2025-08-07**; repository last pushed **2026-05-13**. **License:** the repository has **no `<licenses>` block in pom.xml, no LICENSE file, and no README license statement** → **Unknown from primary sources** (a NIST work, plausibly US-Gov/public-domain, but not stated). For reproducibility, **pin a hosted export bundle** (the exported profile XML checked into the repo) rather than self-hosting; export-versioning metadata is not documented.
**Evidence:** no `<licenses>` block and Spring Boot `data-mongodb`/`data-redis`/`security` starters + module list in [hl7-igamt pom.xml](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/usnistgov/hl7-igamt/master/pom.xml); modules incl. `igamt-hl7-client-v1/v2`, `Dockerfile`, `export`, `verification` in [repo contents](https://api.github.com/repos/usnistgov/hl7-igamt/contents/); "Release 2.11" commit 2025-08-07 in [commits API](https://api.github.com/repos/usnistgov/hl7-igamt/commits); purpose in [hl7-igamt README](https://github.com/usnistgov/hl7-igamt).
**Confidence:** Stack/modules/activity **Verified**; license **Unknown** (no primary license artifact).

#### D6 — Alternatives: Message Workbench, direct profile XML, data-driven checks
**Answer:** (a) Hand-authored `HL7v2xConformanceProfile` XML validated in-process by HAPI (Messaging-Workbench-style) — simplest JVM path, HAPI-level constraints only (D2). (b) Direct profile XML → HAPI `ProfileParser` at runtime. (c) Data-driven Terser-path assertions for predicates/co-constraints HAPI can't express, as a rule layer atop HAPI. Only the NIST/CDC engine natively enforces predicates/co-constraints (D4).
**Evidence:** HAPI profile-XML path in [ProfileParser.java](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/hapifhir/hapi-hl7v2/master/hapi-base/src/main/java/ca/uhn/hl7v2/conf/parser/ProfileParser.java) + [message_profile.xsd](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/hapifhir/hapi-hl7v2/master/hapi-base/src/main/resources/ca/uhn/hl7v2/conf/parser/message_profile.xsd); Terser access in [PipeParser.java](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/hapifhir/hapi-hl7v2/master/hapi-base/src/main/java/ca/uhn/hl7v2/parser/PipeParser.java); NIST predicate/co-constraint enforcement in [content/DefaultValidator.scala](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/usnistgov/v2-validation/master/validation/src/main/scala/hl7/v2/validation/content/DefaultValidator.scala).
**Confidence:** **Inferred** (architecturally supported by verified APIs).

#### D7 — Rule-layer patterns and profile/code seam
**Answer:** Keep base/profile constraints declarative (HAPI `HL7v2xConformanceProfile` or IGAMT bundle) validated by the chosen engine, and put project-specific predicates/co-constraints/business rules in a thin code rule-layer over the parsed message via Terser paths — because HAPI cannot express conditional/co-constraints. Labels map cleanly: engine issues carry constraint IDs; the rule-layer supplies Terser-path locations.
**Evidence:** HAPI predicate gap (comment) in [DefaultValidator.java](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/hapifhir/hapi-hl7v2/master/hapi-base/src/main/java/ca/uhn/hl7v2/conf/check/DefaultValidator.java); Terser access in [PipeParser.java](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/hapifhir/hapi-hl7v2/master/hapi-base/src/main/java/ca/uhn/hl7v2/parser/PipeParser.java).
**Confidence:** **Inferred.**

#### D8 — HL7 tables/local code sets handling
**Answer:** HAPI validates table membership only via a registered `CodeStore` (`CodeStoreRegistry`), keyed by profile+code-system; absent one, checks are silently skipped. NIST/IGAMT handles tables via exported `VALUESETS.xml` + `VALUESETBINDINGS.xml`, giving first-class local value-set support (validated by the `vs` module).
**Evidence:** `getHapiContext().getCodeStoreRegistry().getCodeStore(profileID, codeSystem)` + skip logs in [DefaultValidator.java](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/hapifhir/hapi-hl7v2/master/hapi-base/src/main/java/ca/uhn/hl7v2/conf/check/DefaultValidator.java); value-set files in [CDC lib README](https://github.com/CDCgov/lib-hl7v2-nist-validator); NIST `vs` module (`ValueSetLibraryImpl.scala`) in [validation source dir](https://api.github.com/repos/usnistgov/v2-validation/contents/validation/src/main/scala/hl7/v2/validation).
**Confidence:** **Verified.**

#### D9 — Result formats: stability, de-facto standard vs custom
**Answer:** No single standard. HAPI returns a Java `List<HL7Exception>` (programmatic, stable API, no canonical serialization). NIST/CDC returns a `NistReport` (filtered ERROR/WARNING), a NIST-defined structure atop the `gov.nist.validation.report` library — not a public standard. CI-diffable output requires a custom normalization layer over either.
**Evidence:** HAPI exception-list returns in [DefaultValidator.java](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/hapifhir/hapi-hl7v2/master/hapi-base/src/main/java/ca/uhn/hl7v2/conf/check/DefaultValidator.java); `NistReport` + `gov.nist.validation.report.Entry` in [CDC lib README](https://github.com/CDCgov/lib-hl7v2-nist-validator) and [content/DefaultValidator.scala import](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/usnistgov/v2-validation/master/validation/src/main/scala/hl7/v2/validation/content/DefaultValidator.scala).
**Confidence:** **Verified.**

---

### E. Cross-Cutting (E1–E8 each keyed, confidence per item)

#### E1 — License / transitive flags / copyleft / nonstandard
**Answer:** Apache-2.0 — Synthea, official FHIR validator, HAPI FHIR, SUSHI, Inferno-core, CDC wrapper. **HAPI HL7v2 = dual MPL/GPL with a licensee *choice* of either; GPL is not required** (the POM states "you may choose to use HAPI under the terms of either license"). **NIST `v2-validation` and IGAMT = no LICENSE artifact → Unknown** (likely US-Gov). `FHIRTestResourceGenerator` = NOASSERTION. Mirth Connect = MPL-2.0. Microsoft FHIR-Converter = MIT. Terminology redistribution per C5 table.
**Evidence:** dual-license *choice* text in [HAPI HL7v2 pom.xml](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/hapifhir/hapi-hl7v2/master/pom.xml); CDC Apache-2.0 in [CDC validator LICENSE](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CDCgov/lib-hl7v2-nist-validator/main/LICENSE); HAPI FHIR Apache-2.0 in the [HAPI FHIR license record](https://api.github.com/repos/hapifhir/hapi-fhir/license); no NIST/IGAMT LICENSE in [v2-validation repository contents](https://api.github.com/repos/usnistgov/v2-validation/contents/) and [hl7-igamt pom.xml](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/usnistgov/hl7-igamt/master/pom.xml); Mirth MPL-2.0 in [Mirth LICENSE](https://github.com/nextgenhealthcare/connect/blob/master/LICENSE); FHIR-Converter MIT in the [Microsoft FHIR-Converter repository](https://github.com/microsoft/FHIR-Converter).
**Confidence:** Apache/MPL-GPL-choice/MIT items **Verified**; NIST/IGAMT **Unknown**.

#### E2 — Maintenance (last release/date only; no bus-factor speculation)
**Answer (dated to fetched sources):** Official FHIR validator — **6.9.12, 2026-07-15** ([release](https://github.com/hapifhir/org.hl7.fhir.core/releases/tag/6.9.12)). HAPI FHIR — **v8.10.0, 2026-05-21** ([release API](https://api.github.com/repos/hapifhir/hapi-fhir/releases?per_page=1)). HAPI HL7v2 — **v2.6.0, 2025-02-05**; last push 2026-03-26 ([release API](https://api.github.com/repos/hapifhir/hapi-hl7v2/releases?per_page=1)). CDC wrapper — **1.4.0, 2025-08-29** ([releases API](https://api.github.com/repos/CDCgov/lib-hl7v2-nist-validator/releases)). NIST v2-validation — engine **v1.6.10, 2025-03-07** ([README](https://github.com/usnistgov/v2-validation)). IGAMT — commit "Release 2.11" **2025-08-07** ([commits API](https://api.github.com/repos/usnistgov/hl7-igamt/commits)). NIST GVT — gvt-core last push 2026-02-20 ([repo metadata](https://api.github.com/repos/usnistgov/gvt-core)). SUSHI — **3.15.0, 2025-03-28** ([README](https://github.com/FHIR/sushi)). Synthea — release **v4.0.0, 2026-03-05** ([releases API](https://api.github.com/repos/synthetichealth/synthea/releases?per_page=2)). Inferno-core — last push 2026-07-21 ([repo metadata](https://api.github.com/repos/inferno-framework/inferno-core)). Mirth — last push 2024-09-23 ([repo metadata](https://api.github.com/repos/nextgenhealthcare/connect)). *No steward/bus-factor claims are made beyond these release dates.*
**Confidence:** **Verified** (release dates); qualitative cadence intentionally omitted.

#### E3 — Maven Central / build source
**Answer:** On Central — `ca.uhn.hapi:hapi-base` ([search](https://search.maven.org/solrsearch/select?q=g:ca.uhn.hapi+AND+a:hapi-base&rows=3&wt=json)), `ca.uhn.hapi.fhir:org.hl7.fhir.validation.cli`, HAPI FHIR ([core README](https://github.com/hapifhir/org.hl7.fhir.core)). **Not on Central** — NIST engine (NIST Nexus, SSL-insecure fetch) and thus the CDC wrapper's NIST deps ([CDC lib README](https://github.com/CDCgov/lib-hl7v2-nist-validator)). Synthea — fat JAR via GitHub releases. SUSHI — npm. Microsoft FHIR-Converter — .NET/NuGet. Mirth — GitHub build.
**Confidence:** **Verified.**

#### E4 — Java minimum / module / native issues
**Answer:** Java 11+ — Synthea, HAPI HL7v2, official validator (JDK 11/17 tested); validator `-native` "not presently supported." NIST engine — Scala on JVM. SUSHI — Node 18 (non-JVM). Microsoft FHIR-Converter — .NET (non-JVM). IGAMT — JVM (Spring Boot) + MongoDB/Redis + Angular.
**Evidence:** Java 11 min in [Synthea README](https://github.com/synthetichealth/synthea) and [org.hl7.fhir.core README](https://github.com/hapifhir/org.hl7.fhir.core); JDK 11/17 + `-native` in [confluence](https://confluence.hl7.org/spaces/FHIR/pages/35718580/Using+the+FHIR+Validator); Node 18 in [SUSHI README](https://github.com/FHIR/sushi); .NET in [FHIR-Converter README](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/microsoft/FHIR-Converter/main/README.md).
**Confidence:** **Verified.**

#### E5 — Clojure ergonomics (static/builder/global state/threads/singletons/REPL)
**Answer:** **HAPI FHIR `FhirContext`** is "an expensive object to create … create it once and keep it around" (a version-specific factory/cache) — model as a `defonce` singleton; parsers are lightweight and disposable. **Official validator library** has **shared mutable global state** (cached `StructureDefinition`) with a live concurrency bug (#2470) — from Clojure, prefer the **subprocess** or single-threaded use, and avoid sharing a validation engine across threads in a long-lived REPL. **Synthea** exposes plain `Generator` + `ExporterRuntimeOptions` (constructor + options object) — clean interop. **HAPI HL7v2** uses a `HapiContext`/`PipeParser`/`Terser` object model — straightforward but stateful; construct per-thread or guard. **NIST engine** is Scala with `Future`-based async APIs (see `checkContent(...): Future[Seq[Entry]]`) — usable from Clojure but requires bridging Scala `Future`/collections.
**Evidence:** FhirContext reuse in [HAPI intro docs](https://hapifhir.io/hapi-fhir/docs/getting_started/introduction.html); shared-state bug in [issue #2470](https://github.com/hapifhir/org.hl7.fhir.core/issues/2470); Synthea `Generator(int, long, long)` in [Generator.java](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/synthetichealth/synthea/master/src/main/java/org/mitre/synthea/engine/Generator.java); Scala `Future` API in [content/DefaultValidator.scala](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/usnistgov/v2-validation/master/validation/src/main/scala/hl7/v2/validation/content/DefaultValidator.scala).
**Confidence:** **Verified** (context/state facts); interop effort **Inferred**.

#### E6 — Resource profile: startup / memory / throughput for EVERY candidate
**Answer:** No fetched primary source publishes startup, memory, or throughput figures for any candidate. All entries are **Unknown** and must be measured (EXP-E6). Do not use invented numbers.

| Official FHIR validator (subprocess) | Unknown (JVM + IG-package load) | Unknown | Unknown | No figures in [confluence](https://confluence.hl7.org/spaces/FHIR/pages/35718580/Using+the+FHIR+Validator) |

#### E7 — Determinism hazards
**Answer:** Validator concurrency race (#2470, non-deterministic exceptions, open); terminology-cache poisoning (#2524, open); locale-sensitive behavior (#2462, open — pin `-Duser.language`); Synthea wall-clock default seeds and default thread pool = CPU count; HAPI HL7v2 canonicalizing encode; NIST engine `Future`-based concurrency (order of async detections).
**Evidence:** [#2470](https://github.com/hapifhir/org.hl7.fhir.core/issues/2470), [#2524](https://github.com/hapifhir/org.hl7.fhir.core/issues/2524), [#2462](https://github.com/hapifhir/org.hl7.fhir.core/issues/2462); Synthea defaults in [Generator.java](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/synthetichealth/synthea/master/src/main/java/org/mitre/synthea/engine/Generator.java); HAPI encode in [PipeParser.java](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/hapifhir/hapi-hl7v2/master/hapi-base/src/main/java/ca/uhn/hl7v2/parser/PipeParser.java).
**Confidence:** **Verified.**

#### E8 — Failure behavior offline / malformed / version mismatch
**Answer:** Official validator degrades to **failures** (not skips) offline for terminology-bound checks (#2272) and can report indeterminate warnings for unknown code systems (C5); HAPI table checks **silently skip** without a CodeStore (D2/D8); CDC wrapper needs cached NIST jars or the build fails; version mismatch between validator and IG packages surfaces as canonical/clash errors (see the package-URL exact-match fix referenced in the validator issue tracker). Malformed-input behavior per engine (HAPI throws `HL7Exception`; validator emits OperationOutcome errors) is deterministic given pinned versions.
**Evidence:** offline failures in [#2272](https://github.com/hapifhir/org.hl7.fhir.core/issues/2272); HAPI skip logs in [DefaultValidator.java](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/hapifhir/hapi-hl7v2/master/hapi-base/src/main/java/ca/uhn/hl7v2/conf/check/DefaultValidator.java); NIST Nexus dependency in [CDC lib README](https://github.com/CDCgov/lib-hl7v2-nist-validator).
**Confidence:** **Verified** (offline/skip behavior); malformed-input specifics **Inferred**.

---

### F. Composition / Prior Art

#### F1 — Prior-art toolkits (deepened; language/license/deploy/embeddability/failure)
**Answer:** No single OSS toolkit combines generation + labelled mutation + validation gating in an embeddable JVM form. Deepened survey:
- **Inferno (inferno-framework)** — **Ruby + Docker**, **Apache-2.0**, runnable locally/CI, ONC certification test kits; **not JVM-embeddable**, not a generator/mutator. Failure vs constraints: **language (Ruby) + service architecture** ([inferno-core Apache-2.0/Ruby, last push 2026-07-21](https://api.github.com/repos/inferno-framework/inferno-core); [eCQI Inferno](https://ecqi.healthit.gov/tool/inferno)).
- **HL7 Touchstone (Aegis)** — **hosted web/API**, free for HL7 work, **not open-source, not locally embeddable**. Failure: **hosted/closed** ([tools index](https://www.yourdata.health/testing-tools)).
- **IHE Gazelle** — test-bed infrastructure; its **Schematron Validator is a web service (Saxon-based)** for CDA/HL7v3, not an embeddable library. Failure: **service, not library** ([Gazelle Schematron Validator, Saxon 9HE](https://interoperable-europe.ec.europa.eu/collection/interoperability-test-bed-repository/solution/gazelle-schematron-validator)).
- **NIST GVT / TCAMT / IGAMT** — Java, service-based (GVT is a multi-repo suite, gvt-core last push 2026-02-20; IGAMT is Spring Boot per D5). Embeddable pieces exist (the NIST `v2-validation` engine, D4) but the tools themselves are web apps. Failure: **service deployment + no LICENSE artifact** ([gvt-core metadata](https://api.github.com/repos/usnistgov/gvt-core); [hl7-igamt pom.xml](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/usnistgov/hl7-igamt/master/pom.xml)).
- **Newer/adjacent:** Microsoft FHIR-Converter (.NET, MIT — conversion only, D4 adjacent) and Mirth Connect (Java, MPL-2.0 — integration engine, not a test toolkit).
**Confidence:** **Verified** (per-tool language/license/deploy/failure); GVT/TCAMT license **Unknown**.

#### F2 — Composition pipelines and every conversion/artifact seam
**Answer:** Natural composition: **Synthea (FHIR) → [seam 1: FHIR→v2 projection via HAPI in a PatientExporter plugin] → HL7v2 → [seam 2: labelled mutation] → gates** (FHIR: `validator_cli` with pinned IG packages + local terminology; v2: HAPI `DefaultValidator` and/or NIST/CDC with IGAMT XML; C-CDA later: Saxon Schematron). **Artifact seams to pin:** (a) Synthea seed+version+config; (b) FHIR IG NPM packages `id#version` + local terminology; (c) HAPI/IGAMT profile artifacts; (d) NIST engine jars (Nexus-sourced); (e) validator binary version. Each seam is a version-skew and license boundary.
**Evidence:** exporter seam in [Custom Exporters wiki](https://github.com/synthetichealth/synthea/wiki/Custom-Exporters); FHIR package seam in [packages spec](https://hl7.org/fhir/packages.html); v2 profile seam in [CDC lib README](https://github.com/CDCgov/lib-hl7v2-nist-validator) + [ProfileParser.java](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/hapifhir/hapi-hl7v2/master/hapi-base/src/main/java/ca/uhn/hl7v2/conf/parser/ProfileParser.java).
**Confidence:** **Inferred** (composition from verified component seams).

#### F3 — C-CDA forward compatibility and foreclosures
**Answer:** **Not foreclosed.** C-CDA validation is **XSD (CDA_SDTC) + Schematron**; official C-CDA Schematron is HL7-published and executes **on the JVM via Saxon** (as Gazelle's validator does). OSS C-CDA validators are otherwise **Node.js** (`jddamore/ccda.io`, MIT) or **proprietary-to-self-host** (Lantana). Plan a **Saxon-based Schematron runner** with pinned `.sch` artifacts.
**Evidence:** schema+Schematron model in [C-CDA companion guide](https://build.fhir.org/ig/HL7/CDA-ccda-2.1-sd/understanding_c-cda_and_the_c-cda_companion_guide.html); Saxon 9HE in [Gazelle Schematron Validator](https://interoperable-europe.ec.europa.eu/collection/interoperability-test-bed-repository/solution/gazelle-schematron-validator); [jddamore/ccda.io MIT/Node](https://github.com/jddamore/ccda.io); [Lantana free tools](https://www.lantanagroup.com/resources/free-tools/).
**Confidence:** **Verified** (mechanism + JVM path); tool choice **Inferred**.

#### F4 — Reproducibility-manifest prior art
**Answer:** **No cross-tool (generation+mutation+validator) reproducibility-manifest standard exists.** Reusable building blocks: FHIR **`package.json` pinned dependencies** (`id#version`, no wildcards) + optional `.index.json`; **npm-style lockfiles** (`package-lock.json`, `npm ci --frozen-lockfile`) — the closest reproducible-restore pattern, applicable to SUSHI; container image SHA256 pinning; Synthea's seed+version identity statement. A project-specific manifest {Synthea version+seeds+config hash; IG package set + terminology hashes; profile-artifact versions; validator binary version+flags; package/terminology cache digests} would be novel prior-art-informed work.
**Evidence:** FHIR pinning/`.index.json` in [packages spec](https://hl7.org/fhir/packages.html); lockfile/frozen-install practice in [npm package-lock docs](https://docs.npmjs.com/cli/v11/configuring-npm/package-lock-json/); Synthea identity in [Basic Setup wiki](https://github.com/synthetichealth/synthea/wiki/Basic-Setup-and-Running); package/terminology cache separation in [FilesystemPackageCacheManager.java](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/hapifhir/org.hl7.fhir.core/master/org.hl7.fhir.utilities/src/main/java/org/hl7/fhir/utilities/npm/FilesystemPackageCacheManager.java).
**Confidence:** **Verified absence** of an integrated standard; building blocks **Verified**.
